# Citation reachability and identity audit (VAL-AUDIT-008)

## Technology.org withdrawal and first-party replacements (2026-09-24)

The active `technology-org-deployed-2026` member was withdrawn after its
September 23 direct-origin HTTP 403. No exception, successful retry, invented
passage, or alternative path to that article was asserted. The three new
active entries below are document-specific replacements, not aliases:
Agility's undated homepage (fetched 2026-09-24T04:11:55.369Z;
`source-003.txt`, SHA-256 `552878a2fe5aa1cdd883a72b0d1065e256b51da9d30f2ba5bd66d2bcaa849e51`) states **65,000 hours**,
without a facility allocation; Figure's November 19, 2025 company report
(fetched 2026-09-24T04:12:54.568Z; `source-004.txt`, SHA-256
`b4aa1db3a27b474fb4c34d25751111d535b444d7745c7def88f243a7c76287e0`) reports **1,250+ hours and 90,000+ parts**,
while 84 seconds and >99% are targets; Tesla's Q1 2026 update
(`source-007.txt` extracted from the PDF fetched with verified TLS
2026-09-24T04:58:45.081875953Z, extracted-text SHA-256
`740606cc2d80c0ba00c1e1403b16b32d33204df8c2b0b737d93741c9642e2bc1`) lists California and Texas Optimus lines under
**Construction**, not a built-unit count. The Unitree source artifact was
empty and supports no shipment or rank. These retained source-body checks
are not new network requests, a full-registry citation sweep, or independent
acceptance. Source packets are retained in the existing project at
`validation/brand-v2-editorial/source-recovery-20260906/final-two-citation-ready-20260924/`.

For lossless audit history, the prior active-table row is copied *verbatim*
here, outside the active per-entry table:

## Current Control citation correction (2026-09-24)

The inaccessible `astrom-murray-2008` member has no active product use and
was removed from the registry and the active per-entry table (432 current
members). The September 23 fetch-failure observation remains in the
historical JSON rows below: status 0 was not an origin response. Its retained
first-edition chapter did print the old >95% claim but attributed it to
`[DM02]`; it is not a replacement for the unreachable whole-book citation.
Control no longer presents the percentage, the many-without-D qualifier or
the Åström metaphor. Native originals 1 and 2 use independently checked
active-page removal receipts; original 3 now uses the actual 1942
Ziegler–Nichols text and original 7 uses Tedrake's chapter 8. The
`kalman-1960` catalogue/reprint member stays registered as historical
bibliography, but Control no longer claims that it proves priority or a 1960
LQR Stat. Existing Kalman reprint title/year exception is unchanged.

No whole-registry network run occurred. The Technology.org HTTP-403 observation
remains in the historical row above but is no longer an active registry
verdict; the content/release gate remains red for other outstanding work.
Prior totals and attempts below are dated historical observations, not a
new green check.

First audit: 2026-08-16, covering the 300 entries the registry held then.
Re-audited in full on 2026-09-06 against all 412 entries; the per-entry
table below and the section "Re-audit of the full registry (2026-09-06)"
carry that run. `npm run check:audit-coverage` now reconciles the table
against `data/citations.ts` on every build, so the scope of this ledger
can no longer drift behind the registry unnoticed.

Tool: `npm run check:citations` (`scripts/check-citations.ts`). For every
registry entry it fetches the URL with a real browser user agent, records the
full redirect chain, extracts the fetched document's title (HTML title tag,
`citation_title`/`og:title` meta, or the PDF's first page via pdftotext), and
compares it against the registry entry's title for plausibility. A URL that
returns 200 but serves a different document fails the run; that title check
is the point of the audit. Bot-walled DOI entries are verified through
Crossref metadata (title and year must match); genuinely machine-unverifiable
entries need a documented exception in `data/link-check-exceptions.ts`.

To re-run and regenerate the table below:

```
npm run check:citations                 # human-readable report, exit 1 on any failure
npm run check:citations -- --json       # machine-readable
npm run check:citations -- --markdown   # the table below, minus the action column
```

The command is registered in the mission manifest (`services.yaml` →
`check-citations`) so later per-domain audits can re-run it. It is
deliberately NOT a build gate: 300 network fetches would make every build
slow and flaky offline.

## Result of this run

Checked 300 entries: 287 ok (title verified against the fetched document; 45
of those through Crossref metadata behind publisher bot-walls or JS-shell
pages), 2 title unavailable, 0 title mismatches, 0 dead links, 0 blocked, 0
errors, 11 covered by documented exceptions. Exit code 0.

## Corrections made in this audit (registry edits)

- **vla-perf-2026**: the registry carried a descriptive label ("VLA-Perf:
  Systematic Latency Analysis...") and "NVIDIA Research" as the author. The
  live arXiv page (2602.18397) titles the paper "How Fast Can I Run My VLA?
  Demystifying VLA Inference Performance with VLA-Perf" by Wenqi Jiang,
  Jason Clemons, Karu Sankaralingam and Christos Kozyrakis. Both corrected.
  This is exactly the wrong-document/wrong-metadata class the audit exists
  to catch: the URL was reachable all along.
- **mem-2026**: registry title "VLAs with Long and Short-Term Memory" was a
  paraphrase. The PDF's title page reads "MEM: Multi-Scale Embodied Memory
  for Vision Language Action Models". Corrected.
- **mishra-1987**: registry title said "Multifingered"; the Springer/Crossref
  record for doi 10.1007/BF01840373 says "multifinger". Corrected.
- **mujoco-2012**: url was the IEEE Xplore page, a JS shell that serves no
  title in raw HTML. Moved to the DOI (https://doi.org/10.1109/IROS.2012.6386109),
  which the checker verifies through Crossref.
- **astrom-murray-2008**: url was https://fbsbook.org/, which now 301-redirects
  through four hops to the FBS wiki home page ("FBSwiki"). Moved to the wiki's
  page for the book itself, which carries the book's title.
- **1x-neo-2026 / bd-atlas-2026**: registry titles were our descriptive
  labels ("1X NEO Product Page", "Atlas Product Page") rather than the pages'
  titles. Aligned to the live pages ("NEO Home Robot", "Atlas Humanoid
  Robot").
- Test coupling updated in the same change: `tests/e2e/control.spec.ts`
  asserted the old fbsbook.org href for the Åström chip, and
  `lib/pendulum.ts` documented it; both now point at the fbswiki book page.

## Documented exceptions added (data/link-check-exceptions.ts)

- **kalman-1960** (title-mismatch): the cited DOI is the 2009 Wiley Online
  Books chapter republication, so Crossref reports 2009 against the
  registry's 1960 (the original Bol. Soc. Mat. Mexicana paper). Title matches
  exactly; same reprint-vs-original pattern as ziegler-nichols-1942.
- **levenberg-1944** (title-mismatch): AMS serves the journal masthead
  ("Quarterly of Applied Mathematics") as the page title. Crossref confirms
  title and 1944 year.
- **knowledge-insulation-2025** (title-mismatch): the pi.website research
  page's title tag is the note's tagline; the page body names "Knowledge
  Insulation" five times.
- **gtsam-2026** (title-mismatch): gtsam.org's title tag is a tagline; the
  page body states "Georgia Tech Smoothing and Mapping". Corrected
  2026-10-07: the body statement does not reproduce. The live title is
  "GTSAM 4.3 | GTSAM", and the expansion appears in the borglab/gtsam
  README heading ("GTSAM: Georgia Tech Smoothing and Mapping Library"),
  which the exception now records.
- **maestro-tavac-2023** (blocked): sages.org serves a Cloudflare challenge
  to curl, node fetch and headless Chromium alike. Document identity
  confirmed through the search index (exact URL, 2023-01-31, authors
  "Ruben D. Salas Parra MD and David Pechman MD, FACS" match the registry).
- **mcgee-schmidt-1985** (error): ntrs.nasa.gov suffered a host-side outage
  across the whole audit window (TCP never opens; DNS resolves). The URL was
  fetched live by the liveness sweep earlier the same day, and the search
  index confirms the document. Remove the exception once NTRS answers
  machine clients again.

## Consolidation update (2026-08-18, audit-ledger-consolidation)

- **mcgee-schmidt-1985: exception removed.** NTRS answers machine clients
  again; `npm run check:links` and `npm run check:citations -- --id
  mcgee-schmidt-1985` both pass it unaided (title verified, live fetch,
  run 2026-08-18), so the exception entry was deleted from
  data/link-check-exceptions.ts exactly as its own instruction required.
- The four title-mismatch exceptions (kalman-1960, levenberg-1944,
  knowledge-insulation-2025, gtsam-2026) remain in force: the failure modes
  they cover are invisible to the liveness sweep, and the audit checker
  still needs them. Their entries were re-dated 2026-08-18 with fresh
  verification. A liveness-sweep bug that reported these as STALE (because
  the URL is HTTP-200) was fixed: title-mismatch exceptions are now exempt
  from the liveness sweep's staleness check, since only the audit checker
  can see their failure mode.
- **sutton-bitter-lesson-2019**: added as an error exception on 2026-08-18;
  archive.org intermittently resets TLS connections from this network
  (4 consecutive resets observed) while the capture itself is live
  (independent fetch returned the full essay).

## Honest gaps (title unavailable, not failures)

- **lavalle-1998**, **lavalle-kuffner-2001**: the author-hosted PDFs on
  lavalle.pl use early-LaTeX Type 3 fonts with no usable text layer; pdftotext
  output in every mode is symbol soup, so no title can be extracted and
  compared. The URLs are the canonical author copies and fetch fine (HTTP 200,
  application/pdf); only the title comparison is impossible. Recorded here
  rather than papered over. For **lavalle-kuffner-2001** the registered URL is
  LavKuf01b.pdf since the 2026-09-17 identity correction below (the prior
  LavKuf01.pdf served a different paper but the same Type 3 gap); identity was
  verified through the OCR channel instead of the text layer.

## http-only sources: the archival-capture policy

The registry requires https, and that rule stands. The sanctioned escape
hatch for a canonical source that is genuinely http-only is a DATED
web.archive.org capture (itself https), per the decision recorded 2026-08-12
in `library/content-quality.md` (mission library), with the worked case being
Sutton's The Bitter Lesson. As of this audit the pattern is now encoded in
three places:

1. `data/schemas/citation.ts` documents the pattern and rejects undated or
   wildcard archive URLs (a dated capture is content-addressed and stable;
   `https://web.archive.org/web/*/...` is neither).
2. The `data/citations.ts` registry header documents it for entry authors.
3. `scripts/check-citations.ts` recognizes dated captures via
   `parseArchivalCapture` (lib/citation-audit.ts) and checks them like any
   other citation, marking them "archival capture <timestamp>" in the report
   instead of reporting them as oddities.

No blanket http allowlist exists or is permitted. No registry entry currently
uses the pattern; `audit-frontier` adds the Bitter Lesson entry when it links
the competing-theses prose mention, and the checker is ready for it.

## Venue-year duplication note (superseded, verified)

This feature asked for a registry sweep removing venue strings that embed the
entry year (venue "RSS 2023" with year 2023 rendering as "RSS 2023, 2023.").
That report (2026-08-10) was acted on differently one day later: the
convention that landed (2026-08-11, documented in library/design-system.md)
keeps the venue intact in the registry and dedupes in the renderer.
`venueStatesYear` in `data/citations.ts` detects the duplication, and both
the References bibliography (`components/article/references.tsx`) and the
Cite tooltip (`citationMeta`) render the year once. 83 registry entries carry
a venue containing a year; of those, 65 embed the entry's own year and are
deduped at render time, while the rest (venue year differs from entry year,
e.g. "RSS 2025" with year 2024) deliberately render both years, which is
informative. The behavior is pinned by unit tests
(`tests/unit/citations.test.ts`: renders the year once when the venue already
states it; keeps both years when they differ) and by the references e2e
spec. Sweeping the registry now would fight the documented convention and
change 65 entries for no reader-visible difference, so it was not done.

## Re-audit of the full registry (2026-09-06)

The 2026-08-16 table covered 300 entries. The registry reached 412 while
the table stayed where it was, so 112 entries had never been fetched and
compared against the document they name, and one row
(`lin-humanoid-sim2real-2025`) still recorded a URL the registry had since
replaced with its `v1` form. Nothing detected either, because the ledger's
scope was a sentence rather than a derived number. It is derived now:
`lib/audit-citation-coverage.ts` reconciles `data/citations.ts` against the
table below, `scripts/check-audit-coverage.ts` runs it inside
`npm run validate:content`, and a registry entry with no row fails the
build.

`npm run check:citations` was then run over the whole registry on
2026-09-06. Checked 412: 378 ok (73 of them through Crossref metadata
behind publisher bot-walls or JS-shell pages), 5 title unavailable, 4 title
mismatch, 0 dead, 3 blocked, 3 error, 19 covered by documented exceptions,
2 archival captures. Exit code 1. Of the 112 rows added below, 107 record a
passing first check and 5 are among the ten unresolved entries listed next;
the other five unresolved entries were already in the table.

### The ten entries this run could not settle

None is a dead link. They are named here rather than excepted, because an
exception is a claim about a document and each of these needs a check this
run did not make. They are the open remainder of VAL-AUDIT-008 and are
owned by `npm run check:citations`, which is not a build gate.

**A year disagreement reported as a title mismatch (1).**
`hinterstoisser-2012` fetched a title byte-identical to the registry's, and
the checker's own note gives the real disagreement: Crossref reports 2013
for `10.1007/978-3-642-37331-2_42` against the registry's 2012. That is the
LNCS proceedings volume year against the conference year, the same
reprint-vs-original pattern already excepted for `kalman-1960` and
`ziegler-nichols-1942`. Worth knowing before anyone reads the row: the
`MISMATCH:` cell quotes a title that in fact matches exactly, so the verdict
label is misleading about which field disagreed.

**The page title is not the document title (3).** `gsn-standard-v3`
(scsc.uk serves "Download"), `symbotic-10k-2025` (EDGAR serves the filename
"sym-20250927"), and `seeed-so-arm101-pro-2026`, where the page titles
itself "SO-101 3D-Printed Robotic Arm Frame" while the registry entry names
"SO-ARM101 Pro Kits". The first two look like the title-extraction gap the
existing exceptions cover. The third does not: the fetched title names a
different product tier, so it needs a look at the page itself before anyone
decides whether the URL or the registry label is wrong.

**The host did not answer this machine (3).** `ng-reward-shaping-1999`
(people.eecs.berkeley.edu), `astrom-murray-2008` (fbswiki.org), and
`mcgee-schmidt-1985` (ntrs.nasa.gov) each failed twice on 2026-09-06, on
the full sweep and again on a single-entry re-run, while 378 other entries
fetched normally in the same window. NTRS has done this before; the
2026-08-16 audit recorded an exception for it and removed the exception on
2026-08-18 when NTRS came back.

**A bot wall with no Crossref record to fall back on (3).**
`technology-org-deployed-2026`, `a3-orders-2025` and
`kroger-ocado-closures-2025` all return 403 to a machine client. These are
press pages with no DOI, so the Crossref path that resolves the 40 other
403s in this run cannot resolve them.

## NASA availability lesson registration, 2026-09-22

Retained primary identity and text review only; no fresh URL/liveness acceptance. The official LLIS Subject is Availability Prediction and Analysis. Exact Lesson Info: "Lesson Number841Lesson Date1994-12-01Submitting Organizationjsc". Register NASA as institutional publisher and 1994 as the lesson year, not the original memorandum date or retrieval year. The generic scraper title "Llis" is not the document title.

Observed retrieval: 2026-09-22T22:28:18.417Z, rune___web_scrape / Firecrawl response event 4fea48b3. No origin HTTP status was exposed; no redirect chain is inferred. Requested/final URL: https://llis.nasa.gov/lesson/841. Retained response validation/brand-v2-editorial/source-recovery-20260906/convergence-industrial-primary-bodies-20260922/source-3.txt; SHA256 40f19efe4b87d4195a4e1a31257c8b5b30391019038f6b9a96e94769d0c483f6; markdown SHA256 27b4ccd2a68179b1b8a23adb6d9f0fe65f4281652c71ac459b0d78d30a2e8cae; truncated=false, chars=14844. Current integration rechecked these hashes and the literal passages below; zero requests. Equation images and linked text descriptions were not retrieved. No formula, MTBF estimator, local calculator or whole-record proof is inferred.

> Lesson Number841Lesson Date1994-12-01Submitting Organizationjsc

> This Lesson Learned is based on Maintainability Technique number AT-3 from NASA Technical Memorandum 4628, Recommended Techniques for Effective Maintainability.

> One basic measure of availability, called inherent availability, is useful during the design process to assess design characteristics. The measure involves only the as-designed reliability and maintainability characteristics and can be calculated using the estimated mean-time-between-failure (MTBF) and mean-time-to repair (MTTR) parameters.

> The MTTR time in the inherent availability calculation does not include such times as administrative or logistic delay time, which generally are beyond the control of the designer, and does not include preventive maintenance time.

> A third basic measure of availability, operational availability, considers all repair time: corrective and preventive maintenance time, administrative delay time, and logistic support time.

> where Uptime is the total time a system is in an operable state, and Downtime is the total time the system is in an inoperable state. The sum of Uptime and Downtime, or Total Time, is usually known, specified as a requisite operating time, or is a given time to perform a critical function.

> Estimate or predict the future availability of a system, function, or unit where availability is defined as the probability that the system, function, or unit will be in an operable state at a random time.

Abstract: "the simultaneous methods are more resistant to rotation noise, whereas the separate methods are better at dealing with translation noise. These findings suggest that those conditions should be considered when benchmarking algorithms or performing a calibration process". Body: "we used a UR5e robot arm". |

The drafting lane fetched each source and recorded its passage in the draft's citations file; a second lane re-opened every cited source and logged each fix in its verification report. Per the 2026-10-06 directive this pass re-verifies only what a verification report leaves open; such re-checks are named in the row. Each Note carries the recorded evidence verbatim.

## Classical domain pass: exception evidence, 2026-10-07

- **opencv-hand-eye-docs-2026** (blocked): docs.opencv.org redirects the 4.x
  URL to the 4.13.0 path and answers HTTP 403 there to both checkers
  (`scripts/check-citation-links.ts` reports it BLOCKED and
  `scripts/check-citations.ts` fails it, each run with `--id` on
  2026-10-07). An independent fetch of the exact URL the same day returned
  HTTP 200, title "OpenCV: Camera Calibration and 3D Reconstruction", with
  the calibrateHandEye() passage quoted in the row above. The exception is
  now in data/link-check-exceptions.ts; the row's verdict stays ok.
- **gtsam-2026** (title-mismatch): re-documented against the borglab/gtsam
  README (see "Documented exceptions added"). `scripts/check-citations.ts
  --id gtsam-2026` reports it as a documented exception.
- **whitney-1969** (data/crossref-author-exceptions.ts): the kinematics
  audit noted that the entry for the registry's "Daniel E. Whitney" cited
  only Crossref, which prints "Daniel Whitney". IEEE Xplore's record for
  the paper (document 4081862, exact title), read 2026-10-07, prints
  "Daniel E. Whitney" of MIT's Department of Mechanical Engineering, and
  the entry now records it. `scripts/check-crossref-authors.ts --id
  whitney-1969` reports no divergence before and after the change.

Sources for the two overdue KOL intake notes (filename date 2026-10-05 or earlier) that carry a checkable technical claim: Boston Dynamics' own post on its new Atlas hand, and Runway's two research posts behind The Robot Report's Praxis-1 article. Each page was fetched on 2026-10-09 (HTTP 200) and every cited sentence checked against the quoted passages. The trade-press notes were used as leads only.

Verbatim: "13 degrees of freedom (DOF), directly actuated, built for high fidelity simulation to enable sim-to-real RL", "Atlas' previous seven DOF hands were designed to grasp a large variety of objects", "We have moved to four fingers, including a more dexterous thumb, with a total of 13 DOFs", "dense pressure tactile sensors that cover the fingertips and palm", "At the end of the experiment we had agreement: no pinky", "an extra finger means three more actuators" and "makes it possible to simulate the hand with high dynamic fidelity. ## KOL intake: queue notes older than 72 hours, 2026-10-12

Sources for the twenty KOL intake notes, published more than 72 hours before 2026-10-12 04:25 UTC, whose primary source carries a checkable technical claim. The last seven rows were added after a review of the first decision on the same day: their full texts were read and each carries a checkable result or, for trustworthy-physical-ai-2026, an argued position stated as the authors' view. Nineteen sources are arXiv preprints, whose abstract page and PDF were both fetched on 2026-10-12 (HTTP 200); exploy-2026 is the RAI Institute's own blog post, fetched the same day (HTTP 200). Every cited sentence was checked against the quoted passages. Abstract passages are quoted from the arXiv abstract page and full-text passages from the PDF. The intake summaries, and the IEEE Spectrum pages behind two notes, were used as leads only.

Abstract: "Mulligan makes the initial-state distribution a decision, starting each round's episodes at observed failures and untried states", "Across three real-world tasks evaluated on 2,550 held-out, blinded episodes and two simulated tasks, Mulligan outperforms uniform initial-state sampling at matched collection budgets, and combined with value-based action selection, HiL-IDQL+Mulligan, improves final real-task success by 10-34 percentage points" and "With operator interventions, the human-robot team completes 98% of collection episodes". Verbatim: "Exploy (EXport and dePLOY) is an open-source library that packages environment logic and neural network policies into a single, self-contained computational graph", "Traces the environment's observation generation, neural network forward pass, and action processing using PyTorch", "A lightweight C++ runtime built around ONNX Runtime", "By embedding the complete computational graph and configuration metadata into a single .onnx file", "eliminating manual C++ re-implementation of observation and action pipelines", "It offers evaluation tools to verify step-by-step numerical equivalence between original PyTorch policies and exported ONNX artifacts before running code on physical hardware", "Spot Parkour: Boston Dynamics' Spot quadruped utilizing depth-based and recurrent policies via Exploy", "Exploy has also been deployed on Unitree G1 Humanoid & Boston Dynamics' Atlas" and Roadrunner, "A custom multimodal robot". The post reports no measured result, so the sentence describes the tool and its stated uses only. |

## KOL intake: queue notes older than 72 hours, 2026-10-20

Sources for the KOL intake notes of the 2026-10-20 triage batch (notes written to the queue more than 72 hours before 2026-10-20 15:34 UTC) that were integrated. The three arXiv preprints' abstract pages and PDFs and the newsletter post were fetched on 2026-10-20 with a generic browser user agent (all HTTP 200). Every cited sentence was checked against the quoted passages; abstract passages are quoted from the arXiv abstract page and full-text passages from the PDF. The intake summaries were used as leads only. The per-note dispositions are recorded in the mission library file kol-batch-20261020.md.

| ID | URL | Verdict | Identity | Correction | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| paxton-iros-2026 | https://itcanthink.substack.com/p/dexterity-is-the-frontier-thoughts | ok | match | added 2026-10-20 (KOL intake note of Chris Paxton; data-hardware/hardware-taxonomy) | Fetched 2026-10-20 (HTTP 200): Substack post "Dexterity is the Frontier: Thoughts on IROS 2026", datePublished 2026-10-12T14:08:38+00:00; the author names his employer: "My team at Agility Robotics ran a live demo of mobile manipulation at IROS". Passages: "with two fingered grippers, you do not need tactile sensing as much, because an end effector camera can see deformation in the gripper and infer force and touch"; "with multiple fingers, carefully positioning a camera gets harder and harder. Finger positioning will increasingly occlude the contacts we actually care about."; "Practically speaking, this means we might need tactile sensing."; "Now that these are available off-the-shelf, I expect to see many more researchers actually trying these out. It’s a significant step towards making true, human-like embodied dexterity an intelligence problem, instead of a hardware design problem." The page states his view only; the post's self-reported demo rate ("about 70-80% successful") and hand prices are not used. |
| fact-2026 | https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19155 | ok | match | added 2026-10-20 (KOL intake note of Jiajun Wu; data-hardware/data-bottleneck) | Fetched 2026-10-20 (HTTP 200): v1 13 Oct 2026; University of Michigan, Stanford University and Microsoft Research (PDF affiliations). Abstract: "Using as few as 10 robot trajectories with 90 human trajectories for fine-tuning, FACT outperforms both baselines, with success-rate gains of up to 60 percentage points over T-Rex." PDF Sec. III: "Each participant wears a pair of tactile gloves together with three egocentric RGB cameras mounted on the head and both wrists" and "EgoForce, comprising approximately 2,000 hours of human demonstrations across 40k episodes, 1,000 tasks, 120 scenes, and 1,000 demonstrators"; Sec. IV-D: "We pretrain FACT on a mixture of 2,000 hours of EgoForce human demonstrations (Section III) and 100 hours of T-Rex robot teleoperation data"; Sec. V-A: "All policies are evaluated on a bimanual YAM station with two Sharpa Wave hands", "For each evaluation task we collect 10 robot teleoperation trajectories [...] and 90 human demonstrations", "We compare with two baselines, π0.5 [38] and T-Rex [11], each fine-tuned from its public base checkpoint on the same dataset" and "Binary tasks (seven: lab pipetting, test-tube cap opening, microcentrifuge flip-cap opening, shoelace tying, card shuffling, multi-object grasping, screwdriver use) score success over ten rollouts"; Table I, binary-task "Mean 1.4 8.6 14.3 35.7 41.4" (columns π0.5, T-Rex, FACT w/o force pred, FACT w/o track pred, FACT full); "TABLE II: Pretraining recipe ablation on all ten tasks.", row "Open test-tube cap 80 0 20" (columns human w/ force+robot (full), human w/o force+robot, robot only); Appendix: "human w/o force trains on the same human–robot mixture with the human force supervision disabled and the human tactile inputs masked". The abstract's "up to 60 percentage points" is the test-tube cap row of Table I (80 against 20); the page gives the seven-task means instead. |
| dsp-2026 | https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22340 | ok | match | added 2026-10-20 (KOL intake note of Jitendra Malik; data-hardware/data-bottleneck) | Fetched 2026-10-20 (HTTP 200): v1 15 Oct 2026; PDF affiliations "Amazon Frontier AI & Robotics (FAR)" and "University of Maryland, College Park". Abstract: "a robot learns a play policy from minutes of human play through behavior cloning, then deploys it autonomously to collect hours of interaction data to train a world model. During inference, the same play policy then guides model-predictive control (MPC) by proposing candidate action sequences for evaluation under the learned world model." PDF Sec. IV-A: "the hardware platform uses a fixed Sharpa right hand with 22 commanded joints", "hindsight BC, trained from the same 701K transitions as our method after relabeling achieved outcomes" and "goal-conditioned BC, a policy trained from 15-min teleoperation data, same data amount as our human-collected play data"; Sec. IV-B: "Figure 5(a) isolates the value of planning on 128 distant-goal trials", "Hindsight relabeling improves these rates to 25.8/53.9/71.9%, but play-guided MPC reaches 72.7/91.4/96.1%" (at 0.4/0.6/0.8 rad, simulation), "PPO-PM requires 344M frames, more than 490× than ours, to match the same performance" and, on hardware, "play-guided MPC reaches 68.6/90.0/97.1%"; Fig. 5 caption: "performance-matched PPO (PPO-PM) reaches 86.7% after 344M frames, about 490× the budget" (the caption's "real-world" title conflicts with the text, which runs PPO only in simulation; the page puts the PPO comparison in simulation). Discrepancy recorded, not used: the abstract says "In real world experiment, our method outperforms hindsight behavior cloning by 69.6% in success rate", but the full text runs hindsight BC only in simulation ("The remaining simulation baselines are not transferable to hardware"), where 91.4 against 53.9 at 0.6 rad is a 69.6% relative gain. The page gives the simulation rates and the hardware rate separately. |
| pantact-2026 | https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12558 | ok | match | added 2026-10-20 (KOL intake note of Shuran Song; data-hardware/teleop-rigs) | Fetched 2026-10-20 (HTTP 200): v1 8 Oct 2026; PDF affiliations "Stanford University", "Amazon FAR" and "Carnegie Mellon University", with Shuran Song and Guanya Shi as equal advisors. Abstract: "a wearable, robot-free data collection interface that augments egocentric vision and bimanual UMI grippers with whole-body modular contact patches to record body contact locations and states" and "We deploy PanTact on a Unitree G1 humanoid without additional body contact sensors". PDF Sec. 1: "modular contact patches, reconfigurable force-sensing tiles covering the demonstrator’s forearms and knees that provide contact timing, intensity and location". PDF Sec. 6.1 (Grasp-and-Embrace): "We collected 100 demonstrations", "We evaluate each method over 20 rollouts", "Ours (Upper-Body IK) achieves an 85% success rate" and "Humanoid UMI baseline consistently fails to support the bulky object because its whole-body controller tracks only EEF commands". PDF Sec. 6.3 (Carry-and-Push): "We collected 110 demonstrations", "We evaluate each method over 10 rollouts" and "Ours (WBIK+Dense Tracker) achieves a 90% success rate". PDF Sec. 6: "For each task, all methods are trained on the same demonstrations". 17 of 20 and 9 of 10 are those rates over those rollout counts. Not used: the Sweep-and-Stow score, which gives partial credit. |

## KOL intake: overdue queue notes, 2026-10-24

Sources for the KOL intake notes of the 2026-10-24 overdue triage (the four queue notes older than 72 hours named by the owner proxy on 2026-10-23, an exception to the 2026-10-21 pause) that were integrated. Each arXiv preprint's abstract page, HTML full text and PDF were fetched on 2026-10-24 with a generic browser user agent (all HTTP 200). Every cited sentence was checked against the quoted passages; abstract passages are quoted from the arXiv abstract page and full-text passages from the PDF. The intake summaries were used as leads only. The per-note dispositions are recorded in the mission library file kol-batch-20261024.md.

| ID | URL | Verdict | Identity | Correction | Note |

| rax-2026 | https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22496 | ok | match | added 2026-10-24 (KOL intake note of Pieter Abbeel; manipulation/hierarchical) | Fetched 2026-10-24 (HTTP 200): v1 15 Oct 2026; PDF affiliations "1 Harvard University, 2 NVIDIA, 3 UC Berkeley, 4 MIT, 5 Stanford University", Yilun Du (Harvard) last author, Pieter Abbeel (UC Berkeley). Abstract: "a general-purpose coding agent generates robot control code directly, operating without action demonstration data, hand-engineered action primitives, or value-function guidance". App. F: "The average margin of 37.4 points stated in the abstract and the introduction is the mean of the five margins of this chart, 53.4 on RoboDojo, 38.3 on BEHAVIOR-1K, 7.9 on HumanoidArena, 45.5 on the bimanual tasks of DexJoCo, and 42.1 on LIBERO-Pro", against "the strongest prior model, that is, with learned policies such as VLAs and world action models rather than with other agents". Sec. 1: "On HumanoidArena, it sets a new state of the art (+7.9) with three teleoperated demonstrations per task, where every baseline uses 100." App. G: "In every task the agent received only the task text quoted below, typed by the operator, and the robot's tools; it received no demonstrations, no object models, and no trained policy." App. H: "the agents' model calls consumed over 100k US dollars of API credits". The abstract prints the margin as "+37.4%"; App. F defines it in points, and the page says points. |
| coevolve-2026 | https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23288 | ok | match | added 2026-10-24 (KOL intake note filed under Cheng Chi; manipulation/hierarchical) | Fetched 2026-10-24 (HTTP 200): v1 16 Oct 2026 and v2 19 Oct 2026, text identical apart from the arXiv stamp; PDF affiliations "1 Peking University 2 Renmin University of China 3 Beijing XYZ Embodied AI Co., Ltd. 4 Beijing Academy of Artificial Intelligence (BAAI)", corresponding authors Cheng Chi (Renmin University of China) and Hao Tang (Peking University). The intake note attributes the paper to Cheng Chi of Sunday Robotics; the paper's Cheng Chi is a different person, so the page names the institutions only. Abstract: "Physical verification evaluates each executed transition, rejected behaviors trigger structured coordination revision from an accepted checkpoint"; "Across 15 base task runs with three multimodal backbone families, CoEvolve completes 8 tasks". Sec. 4.1: "Two Unitree G1 humanoids jointly carry a freely supported 0.5 kg rigid payload through constrained corridors"; "Each robot uses an independent SONIC deployment with frozen policy weights". Sec. 4.2: "The base task uses an 80-transaction budget"; "differences in runtime and continuation settings make the aggregate comparisons descriptive rather than matched causal estimates". Table 2 (GPT-5.4-mini, n = 5): REMAC, COHERENT, CaP-Agent0 and ASPIRE (Online) each 0/5. |
| stopwatch-2026 | https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23841 | ok | match | added 2026-10-24 (KOL intake note of Pieter Abbeel; data-hardware/evaluation-crisis) | Fetched 2026-10-24 (HTTP 200): v1 16 Oct 2026; PDF affiliations "1 Amazon FAR 2 New York University 3 UC Berkeley 4 Carnegie Mellon University", Xinhao Liu first author, Pieter Abbeel, Guanya Shi and Chen Feng among the advisors. Sec. 3.4: "Since the benchmark (Yokoyama et al., 2024b) credits a stop only when the annotation matches the goal word, we find agents scored a failure at perfectly valid goal instances that carry another name, such as a couch annotated as sofa"; Fig. 5: "a manual audit filters out 23% of the val unseen episodes" (Table 5: 3,000 episodes, 2,309 clean). Sec. 4.3: "The clean protocol credits the expert in 96% of the episodes"; "The official protocol credits it in 72%". Table 1, HM3D-OVON val unseen: Ours 53.9 official and 69.6 clean, LightNav-0 47.5 and 60.8 (leads of 6.4 and 8.8 points). The clean criterion also requires the goal to be clearly visible, so the page says "corrected scoring" and does not attribute the change to the label merge alone. |
